# SPEC 69a — McBylaw Policy: the rulings register (part of Spec 69)

**Status:** RATIFIED (the status column of each row below is authoritative; this file is Spec 69's §1 register,
split out for Spec 69's byte budget — it carries no rule of its own beyond the rows)

> Sibling of `docs/specs/01-pipeline/69_mcbylaw_policy.md` (Spec 69). Created 2026-10-07: Spec 69 had grown to
> 77.6 KB, and its register — one row per ruling plus dated notes, amended only by adding rows — is the part that
> grows. The §1 register arrived here ONLY through `node scripts/analysis/spec-split-check.mjs --refresh` (move
> `M19` in `docs/specs/01-pipeline/122_split_manifest.json`, classification `REGISTER`: live rows, not history),
> and `## 1. The register` survives in Spec 69 as a stub pointing here.

**Next free id:** `M-334` (highest allocated M-333, measured at integrate-25). M-261..M-264, M-269..M-279, M-292..M-294 and M-299..M-304 were reserved for fix round 4D lanes and are unused; they stay free.

**Governance:** these rows ARE Spec 69's rulings. Cite them as before — `Spec 69 M-n` — and amend them here, by
the protocol in Spec 69's header (add a row or a dated note, in §1a below; an id is never reused). Spec 69
keeps the policy narrative, the Phase 1 decisions (§2), by-law 654-2025 (§3), the open questions (§4), the recorded
positions P-1..P-8 (§5) and the glossary (§6). Every program that parses the rulings reads this file and Spec 69
together through `readSpec69Rulings` (`scripts/analysis/bylaw/universe.mjs`), never one file alone.

## Operating Boundaries

### Target Files
- `docs/specs/01-pipeline/69a_mcbylaw_register.md` (this register). The files the rulings govern are listed in Spec 68.

### Out-of-Scope Files
- Everything under `src/`, `scripts/`, `migrations/` — this register rules; Spec 68's plan builds.
- `scripts/enrich-parcels.js` (enrich_parcels): M-176's main-wall max-build change is a Spec 78 amendment built in the DB lane.

### Cross-Spec Dependencies
- **Relies on:** Spec 69 (the protocol and status key in its header); Spec 68 (the standard the rows rule on).
- **Consumed by:** Spec 68; `scripts/analysis/bylaw/universe.mjs` `readSpec69Rulings` (G-PROV ruling-id arm, the
  G-UNIVERSE `--accept` pin); `scripts/analysis/spec-split-check.mjs` (arms i, ii and vi verify the move).


---

## §1. The register (moved from Spec 69 §1) — LIVE REGISTER, 2026-10-07

Moved from Spec 69 — Spec 69 split (QUEUE follow-up 2026-10-07: Spec 69 at 77.6 KB, split the register into 69a like 124→124a). The M-rows and dated notes stay in force and are cited as Spec 69 M-n; programs read 69a + 69 through readSpec69Rulings (scripts/analysis/bylaw/universe.mjs). New rows and dated notes are added in 69a.

| **M-0** | **Simplicity.** Every field, gate, file and mode names the mistake it prevents or the reader it serves; edge cases are not chased; each change says what it replaces. | A table nobody can follow is not trustworthy (operator). | **Process rule — no mechanical gate (declared).** Applied at every plan panel; the plan's fold record is the precedent (v0.5: 24 gates → 13) | RATIFIED 2026-10-06 |
| **M-1** | **The single source of truth is the committed generated JSON** (`docs/reference/bylaw-provisions.json`), not a DB table or a hand-kept doc. | **M-2** | **One row per slicer `regulation_id`; clauses are a generated `clauses[]` sub-array,** not separate rows. | Only the by-law's own numbering makes exhaustiveness measurable; clause scoping makes literal checks exact. | G-TEXT | RATIFIED 2026-10-06 (decision 4) |

| **M-15** | **Ch.900 exception scope: every exception binding ≥ 100 residential lots, counting INHERITED lots, built in two waves** (not a fixed top-N). An exception's lots = its direct lots + the direct lots of every exception that INCLUDEs it, transitively; this decides **membership and rank only**. **Coverage shares use direct lots** (one exception label per lot; 0 multi-x labels in 440,094 *[measured]*). **Direct-lot census:** 515 exceptions ≥ 100 direct lots cover 300,616 of **339,125** excepted residential lots = **88.6 %**; the R −1 sentinel (4,527 lots, `R (d0.6)` with no `x`) is not an exception, so the 343,652 figure that includes it is not a denominator. **Inherited counting** adds RD 1462, RS 336, RT 352 (0 direct lots; 38,512 / 8,249 / 2,323 inherited), which carry the FSI cap. **Wave 1** = top 220 by INCLUDE-closed lots; **wave 2** = the rest. Every other excepted lot is disclosed "site-specific exception not yet captured". Rows are Phase 2. | A direct-lot threshold drops the exceptions carrying the FSI cap *[measured]*. A threshold maintains itself; a top-N does not. | Census render (Phase 1); G-UNIVERSE membership arm (Phase 2) | RATIFIED 2026-10-06 |

Reg. 462/24 restrains by-laws; it imposes no value where the by-law is silent (s.5(2): a by-law that permits more prevails). A provincial unit applies only where the by-law is **more restrictive**, and it is never ADDITIONAL (Spec 68 §7.5 rule 4). Direction is data, `limits_bylaw` per unit, checked against the verbatim text: coverage s.5(1)1 "Up to 45 per cent … is permitted" and FSI s.5(1)2 "there is no limit" are `min_permission` (*verbatim*); separation s.4(1)2 "shall be at least 4 metres" is worded as a provincial minimum, and s.4(2) says only that a by-law permitting *less* prevails, so reading it as a ceiling on what a by-law may require (`max_requirement`) is *inferred* from s.35.1(4), not stated. **Both readings are flagged for the M-29 expert sample.** | | | RATIFIED 2026-10-07 |

| **M-60** | **Evaluator hardening rules (2026-10-07, hardening lane after the Phase 2 red-team and oracle B; provisional and conservative, operator ratification owed).** (a) **Absence (rule 7a)** returns `unregulated` only when no unit of any by-law layer carries the target (keyed or pending) and the lot's exception and every exception in its INCLUDE closure is authored for the target (`authored` / `partial` listing the target, every unit non-pending); otherwise `not_evaluated:exception_not_authored:<exception>` (new closed code). Every `absence-rulings.json` entry is executed against its pinned page, case-insensitive and whitespace-folded, with every occurrence of its occurrence patterns inside a declared accounted occurrence. (b) **Provincial units** come only from `external.json` (`provincialUnits()`); scope is resolved to lot inputs only through `vocab.provincial_scope` (land → `parcel_of_urban_residential_land`; rowhouse → townhouse; houseplexes unmapped; `house_units` / `ancillary_units`); they restrain a more restrictive by-law value per `limits_bylaw`, are never additional, and never switch absence off. (c) **Determinism:** candidates are put in a declared total order (layer, `unit_id`, content); a reason is chosen by a declared priority (building type, then `NOT_EVALUATED_CODES` order, then text); the M-50 type list is sorted. (d) **Trace:** every result records `inputs[]` (each lot input read, conditions included) and the condition values in its trace. (e) **Threshold tokens** declare `reads`; each unit carrying one has a `condition.if` reading one of them. (f) **Undecided applicability** (M-50 generalised): every combination of undecided units (≤ 8) is resolved; the result stands only if all agree, else `not_evaluated` with the priority reason. (g) Argument-level displacement substitutes the displacing value at the higher layer (Spec 68 §7.5 rule 2, the §7.4 12.0 example); `unlimited` and `unregulated` at one layer → `unlimited`. **Held for the operator:** PERMIT vs PROHIBIT at the same rank stays `conflict` (not decided); whether a duplex / triplex is a provincial "detached house"; what lot input proves "urban residential land"; whether an unauthored exception should also stop a base value (red-team A14). | Red-team REDTEAM.md A11–A13, A15, A16, A18, A25 (2 CRITICAL, 5 HIGH silent misses) and oracle B classes C2/C3/C4 *[measured: 650 / 9,156 order-dependent pairs → 0; oracle B agreement 94.21 % → 97.89 %]*. | `bylaw-harden.logic` / `.infra`, G-EVAL | PROPOSED |

| **M-63** | **A closed scenario catalogue; every scenario is computed for every parcel; no user choice.** Axes (Spec 78 §6.2): **P** principal form (detached; detached + secondary suite; detached houseplex 2–4; houseplex 5–6, 600.60 only; semi; semi + suite; semi houseplex; townhouse; townhouse + suite) · **A** ancillary suite (none, garden, laneway; 150.7.20.1(2)) · **K** parking (none, integral, detached; M-66) · **T** tier (as-of-right, CoA; M-65). Each value cites the clause that defines or permits it; the catalogue ⇄ the permission clauses of `scenario-clauses.json`, both ways. Houseplex unit counts are fields inside one scenario where no clause keys the envelope on the count; **the provincial unit-count scope (M-55, M-60 (b)) can split them** (Spec 78 §6.2 C1). A collapse is a declared, **tier-qualified** equality with its evidence, a metamorphic fixture both ways, **and a per-parcel equality check on the trial population**; a collapse across CoA forms with different factors is illegal at the CoA tier. Permission rules (semi / townhouse on a single lot) are not collapses. The catalogue is pinned; changing it is a ruling. **Supersedes P-7 on ratification.** | Operator 2026-10-07 ("no user choice"). (1) **No double counting:** u is the lot-matched realized FSI of CoA builds ÷ as-of-right builds of the same form, fitted against the per-scenario AOR(s) (formula id + evaluator + table version), never today's `opt_aor`; second-look estimates u_single ≈ 1.14, u_multiplex = 1.00, u_single_suite fitted in the trial — **values to be measured (M-70)** on AOR(s). ## §1a. Rows and dated notes added after the move (2026-10-07 onward)

The block above is hash-locked: `spec-split-check` arm (i) fails if one byte of move M19 changes, so it is never
edited in place. A new ruling is a new row in the table below, same columns and status key. A dated note on a row
above is a row below whose first cell is empty and whose text starts `*Dated note <date> (M-n; <source>):*`. Parsers read
this table with the block (`parseRulings` keys on the first cell; an empty first cell carries no id). A dated note may instead be appended inside the row's status cell (M-246, `2026-10-20-chat-batch-c-bylaw#CHAT-14`): dated, naming its source, the prior status text kept as its prefix; it inserts no line, so no later line citation moves.

A clause that permits an encroachment with no stated extent stays `PERMIT`, as the §7.1 example reads. | §7.3 lets a PERMIT carry an expression only for condition literals, and `literal_not_expressed_reason` has no reason for a permitted extent. A PERMIT reading would therefore leave the 1.5 m / 2.5 m / 50 % extents unaccounted for, and G-CLAUSE (Spec 68 §6.4 rule 6) would fail them. `vocab.dsl_target.encroachment_m` (S5, M-56) already declares the concept as "maximum distance a building element may encroach into a required setback" with the pattern "may encroach". | **M-158** | **A lawfully existing building defaults to "new build"; an answer comes with a caveat, and the existing size is an optional input (operator ruling Q-E, 2026-10-15).** Operator, verbatim: *"we still compute the new build but with the caveat that if the existing structure is larger you can build to that size - we dont need an input as a first step. we will allow an input for the existing footprint and stories. we want to provide an answer without it."*<br>**(1) Default.** When no existing-building input is supplied, `lot_condition.lawfully_existing` resolves FALSE: the declared default "new build". The evaluator answers the base limit. On every target where a `lawfully_existing` unit would otherwise apply, it attaches a disclosure caveat **generated, never free text**, from the unit's target and bound (`vocab.lawfully_existing_caveat`). The template is `phrase.template` if set, else `template[bound]`, from four keys:<br>`max` (building subject): "If a building on this lot lawfully existed on \<cut-off\> and it \<phrase\>, that \<measure\> is the limit instead."<br>`max_lot` (lot subject): "If this lot lawfully existed on \<cut-off\> with \<phrase\>, that \<measure\> is the limit instead."<br>`min` (lot subject): "If this lot lawfully existed on \<cut-off\> with \<phrase\>, that \<measure\> is the minimum instead."<br>`min_building` (building subject): "If a building on this lot lawfully existed on \<cut-off\> and it \<phrase\>, that \<measure\> is the minimum instead."<br>\<cut-off\> is "9 May 2013 (or a building permit issued before then)", or "26 June 2025" where M-39 applies (the unit reads `2025-06-26`).<br>**Phrase map (closed; `{v}` = the base limit; target bound phrase / measure [template]):**<br>`height_m` max "is taller than {v} m" / height; `main_wall_height_m` max "has main walls taller than {v} m" / main wall height; `building_depth_m` max "is deeper than {v} m" / depth; `building_length_m` max "is longer than {v} m" / length; `building_width_m` max "is wider than {v} m" / width; `entrance_height_m` max "has its main entrance more than {v} m above established grade" / entrance height; `lot_coverage_pct` max "covers more than {v}% of the lot" / coverage; `height_storeys` max "has more than {v} storeys" / number of storeys; `gfa_m2` max "has more than {v} m² of floor area" / floor area; `fsi` max "has more floor area than FSI {v}" / floor area.<br>`front_setback_m` / `side_setback_m` / `rear_setback_m` min "is closer than {v} m to the front lot line" / "to a side lot line" / "to the rear lot line", measure front / side / rear yard setback [`min_building`]; `separation_m` min "is closer than {v} m to another building on the lot" / separation distance [`min_building`]. For a minimum setback, the lawful relief is a building that stands closer, so the subject is the building.<br>`driveway_width_m` max "a driveway wider than {v} m" / driveway width [`max_lot`]; `parking_spaces` max "more than {v} parking spaces" [`max_lot`] and min "fewer than {v} parking spaces" / number of parking spaces; `loading_spaces` min "fewer than {v} loading spaces"; `bicycle_parking_spaces` min "fewer than {v} bicycle parking spaces"; `parking_rate` min "fewer than {v} parking spaces per dwelling unit (or per the stated base)" / parking rate.<br>`fsi` min "less floor area than FSI {v}"; `front_landscaping_pct` min "less than {v}% of the front yard as landscaping" / landscaping; `front_soft_landscaping_pct` min "less than {v}% of the front yard as soft landscaping" / soft landscaping (Q-O, 2026-10-15, from 900.3.10(5)(C)(ii)); `required_lot_frontage_m` min "less than {v} m of frontage" / frontage; `required_lot_area_m2` min "less than {v} m² of lot area" / lot area.<br>**Constants are vocab, not code:** `token` = `lawfully_existing`, `default` = false, `inputs` = {footprint: `existing_footprint_sqm`, storeys: `current_stories`}, `m39_date` = `2025-06-26`. A target that carries a `lawfully_existing` unit but has no phrase fails validation (`caveat_unmapped`, closed answer set; built by the P2-0 seat in the evaluator, reading these vocab keys). This covers both 800.50(410) ("Lawfully Existing Means: (A) existing lawfully on May 9, 2013 …") and the 2025-06-26 cut-off of 600.60.40(3)(C) (M-39).<br>**(2) Optional inputs.** `existing_footprint_sqm` (new; registered in `vocab.user_input` 2026-10-15) and the existing-storeys input, which is the already-declared `current_stories` (Spec 68 §6 `user_input`; no second name is added). When either is supplied, `lawfully_existing` is true. `existing(\<target\>)` then reads them ONLY for targets they measure directly: `lot_coverage_pct` from footprint ÷ lot area, and storey targets (`height_storeys`) from storeys. Targets with no direct input (`height_m`, `main_wall_height_m`, `building_depth_m`, frontage) keep the caveat. Storeys are never converted to metres.<br>**(3) Join.** The token joins the declared user input: `vocab.lot_condition.lawfully_existing = {user_input: "lawfully_existing_status"}`. So when an input is declared but malformed, the reason is `needs_user_input:lawfully_existing_status`, never `condition_unknown`.<br>**Compliance (one line each):**<br>**68:** a declared default plus a disclosure (§6 `disclosure`; §7.5 rules 1 / 7). No unit or keyed field changes. `existing()` is the §7.4 value form.<br>**69 / 69a:** M-39 (cut-off kept), M-54 (the caveat is not `unregulated`), M-64 (field `computed` plus caveat), M-149 (no new untestable scope: the default makes the scope testable).<br>**122 / 122a:** N/A; no step is created or optimized.<br>**124 / 124a:** N/A now. The evaluator change (P2-0 seat) is pure compute: the default is data, read from vocab and the ruling, never an inline branch (R-BZ).<br>**128:** N/A to the McBylaw table; the surface shows the caveat text as disclosed.<br>**129:** oracle C compares only the base limit; a notice about an existing building is `not_comparable:lot_input_required` (M-121 (a) family).<br>**Five words:** STANDARDIZED, one default and one caveat wording for every lawfully-existing unit, and one storeys input (`current_stories`). OBSERVABLE, the caveat is attached per target and counted. SCALABLE, it needs no per-lot input. ACCURATE, the base limit is never wrong for a new build, and the existing-size relief is disclosed, never guessed or converted. UNDERSTANDABLE, the caveat is one plain sentence.<br>**Nothing hidden:** the default is a declared assumption, visible in this row and in every caveat. It is not a numeric tunable. If it is ever made switchable, the switch belongs in `scripts/seeds/logic_variables.json`, read by `lv()` (Spec 68 §7.4), never inline. | The operator wants an answer on every lot without asking for the existing building first. Answering the new-build limit is never wrong for a new build, and the caveat keeps the larger-existing relief visible instead of guessing it. The inputs refine only what they measure directly. | vocab `lot_condition.lawfully_existing.user_input` and `lawfully_existing_caveat` phrase map (built 2026-10-15; `bylaw-rulings` M-158 test); evaluator default + caveat + `existing_footprint_sqm` vocab registration (owed, P2-0 seat) | RATIFIED 2026-10-15 (operator, Q-E) |
| **M-159** | **A unit governs only its own subject (operator 2026-10-15, WF3 ancillary / other-chapter leak plan, Q-L (a); with Q-M (b)).**<br>**(1) Zone-category scope.** A base unit from a zone-category chapter other than Residential, with empty `application.zones`, applies only to lots in that category. The chapters and their categories are declared in `vocab.zone_category_chapter`: 15 Residential Apartment, 30 Commercial, 40 Commercial Residential, 50 Commercial Residential Employment, 60 Employment Industrial, 80 Institutional, 90 Open Space, 100 Utility and Transportation (the City's chapter titles). `zones` lists the vocab zones each category admits; for every one of them it is [] on MaxBLD residential lots. A chapter not in the map keeps today's behaviour. (The plan's 70 / 105 are not chapters of the pinned table of contents and are not mapped.)<br>**(2) Accessory-structure scope.** A unit whose `application.uses` names an accessory structure (`vocab.accessory_structure_use`: swimming_pool, deck_or_platform, private_garage, energy_device, portable_classroom) governs that structure, never the principal building. On a principal-building target it is set aside, and the answer carries that structure's closed disclosure, "rules for a \<structure\> on this lot are not part of this answer" (one phrase per structure in the vocab), until the structure dimension (phase2-plan-v2 §S.3) answers it.<br>**(3) Specific-use scope.** A unit whose `application.uses` names a specific use (`vocab.specific_use`: `school`, Section 150.48) applies only on a lot that has that use, with the closed disclosure "rules for a school on this lot are not part of this answer". By Q-M (b), the 150.48 rows carry `uses` (M-75 corrections on 15 units). 150.48.50.11(1)(A)/(B) are not keyed: their clause names neither use, so evidence is impossible, and they only disapply the already school-scoped 150.48.50.10. UNDERSTANDABLE, one plain sentence per structure.<br>**Q-K (measured 2026-10-15, no keying needed here):**<br>(C) 10.40.40.20(2)(A)/(C) are already UNUSUAL in this worktree. | **M-172** | **Site facts versus design choices: MaxBLD is theoretical, so a permissive design-choice condition is taken as met and disclosed as how the answer is achieved (operator 2026-10-15, review of 600.60.40(2)(A); refines M-169).** Operator, verbatim: *"We can't know if these conditions have been applied. MaxBuild is theoretical - this is the max build height with a disclosure on how this is achieved."*<br>**(1) Two closed classes.** Every non-threshold, non-generated `lot_condition` token and every `user_input` is in exactly one of: data-filled, scenario-filled (M-160), M-158-specialised, **site fact** (`vocab.undetermined_condition_disclosure`), or **design choice** (`vocab.design_choice_disclosure`). The totality is test-locked.<br>**(2) Site facts** (the builder cannot choose them): `flanking_lot`, `side_yard_abuts_street`, `abuts_lane`, `abuts_named_street`, `shoreline_or_top_of_bank`, `heritage_property`, `adjacent_lot_fronts_flanking_street`, `front_averaging_not_applicable`, `one_` / `two_abutting_buildings_within_15_m`, `parcel_of_urban_residential_land`, `no_rear_lot_line`; inputs `current_stories`, `existing_driveway_type`, `existing_driveway_width_m`, `existing_front_parking_pad`, `pool`; and `lawfully_existing` (M-158). M-169 is unchanged for them: default not holding, plus an "If …" disclosure.<br>**(3) Design choices** (the builder controls them): `lowest_level_joists_1_0_to_1_5_m_for_80_pct`, `lowest_level_ceiling_2_4_m_for_80_pct`, `lowest_level_contains_dwelling_unit`, `has_secondary_suite`, `individual_private_driveway`, `garage_entrance_faces_street`, `all_units_front_street`, `unit_not_fronting_street` (townhouse layout); inputs `roof_type`, `suite_wall_openings`, `bedroom_count`, `has_secondary_suite`. These leave M-169's default-not-holding list.<br>A **permissive** unit (it raises a max or lowers a min) is taken as met, and the answer carries "{measure} {value} is achieved if: {conditions}." For example: "Maximum height 10.5 m is achieved if: the lowest level's ceiling joists are 1.0–1.5 m above grade for 80% of its area; and it has at least 2.4 m floor-to-ceiling height for 80% of its area; and it contains a dwelling unit." A **restrictive** design-choice unit is not applied to the answer; it is shown only as the trade-off, "If the design {conditions}: {effect}."<br>**(4) Gap, stated, not decided:** 600.60.40(2)(A) governs a **houseplex of 5–6 units** (600.60, By-law 654-2025). M-63 lists "houseplex 5–6 (inside 600.60 only)" as a P value. But the Layer 2 v1 projection (M-63 note 2026-10-14) and M-160 carry only the houseplex classes 2–4 (evaluated at 4), and the 600.60 map is not held (M-63 Q3: `not_evaluated:map_area_not_held`). So MaxBLD has **no 5–6-unit class today**. Its height 10.5 m and achieved-by disclosure would surface only once a 5–6 class and the 600.60 map land. That is an operator decision.<br>**Compliance:** **68** §7.5 rule 1 (a declared, class-based default), §6 `disclosure`. **69a** M-169 refined (site facts unchanged), M-158 (specialised), M-160 / M-63 (classes; the gap above), M-149. **122 / 122a** N/A. **124 / 124a**: declared data (R-BZ). **128**: surface text. **129**: oracle C compares base limits only. **Five words:** ACCURATE, a permissive design option is the theoretical maximum and states its cost, while a site fact is never assumed; OBSERVABLE, achieved-by and trade-off disclosures, counted; STANDARDIZED, two closed classes, test-locked; SCALABLE, data; UNDERSTANDABLE, "… is achieved if: …". | MaxBLD answers the most a lot can hold. A design option the owner can choose belongs in that maximum, with its conditions stated. A site fact the owner cannot choose must not be assumed. | vocab `undetermined_condition_disclosure`, `design_choice_disclosure` (`bylaw-rulings` M-169 / M-172 tests); evaluator rendering (P2-0 seat, owed) | RATIFIED 2026-10-15 (operator) |

| **M-175** | **A unit scoped to an accessory-structure use never limits the principal building (operator y 2026-10-15, envelope review).** The operator review found 10.5.60.20(8)(A), the rear yard setback for an outdoor **swimming pool** (25.0 m on a through lot backing onto a frontage; 3.0 m corner; 1.2 m otherwise), deciding the MAIN building's `rear_setback_m` on 14 of the 24 review example lots: `applies()` never read `application.uses`, and 66 units scoped to a `vocab.accessory_structure_use` (pool, private garage, deck or platform, energy device, portable classroom) are keyed to main-building envelope targets (46 of them private-garage side setbacks).<br>**(1) Rule.** A unit whose `application.uses` names a `vocab.accessory_structure_use` is not a candidate for the principal building: `applies()` returns `{ok:false, why:"use <x> (an accessory structure, not the principal building)"}`. It limits that structure, evaluated only when a later phase answers for the structure itself.<br>**(2) Measured effect (review dataset rebuilt 2026-10-15):** 70 table answers on the example lots changed: rear_setback_m 14 (25 m → 7.5 / 8.39 m from the zone rules), side_setback_m 27 and side_setback_street_m 2 (not_evaluated → a value), separation_m 25 (conflict → a value), encroachment_clearance_m 2. `validate.mjs --check` exit 0; bylaw suite 48 files / 1,386 pass; no eval-vector answer moved.<br>**(3) Open, not decided here:** units in the ancillary-building articles (x.x.60) keyed with NO use token still reach the principal building (10.5.60.20(10)(B) now decides an encroachment_clearance_m answer); and 10.5.60.20(8)(A) keys its three arms (i)/(ii)/(iii) as one unit with no per-arm condition. The structure's closed disclosure and the counted `structure_scoped` tally (M-159 OBSERVABLE) are owed at the P2-0 seat with the M-158 caveat rendering; until then the trace names the excluded use.<br>**(5) G-EVAL V12** (RT, building type NULL, `side_setback_m`) now answers 7.5 m: every type agrees (M-50) once the accessory-structure-scoped 10.5.60.20(9)(B) is set aside. Its `not_evaluated` expectation is withdrawn and 7.5 m accepted (operator 2026-10-16, ADJ-3 (a); `eval-vectors.json` V12 adjudication; V12 stays `no_expected:policy_null`); its Spec 67 anchor "NOT 7.5" was written against the retired flat constant. | ACCURATE: a pool or garage setback no longer sets the house's yard; UNDERSTANDABLE: the trace names the excluded use; STANDARDIZED: the use list is vocab data, not code. | `evaluate.mjs` `applies()` + `makeContext().accessoryUses`; `vocab.mjs` `evaluatorVocab()` carries `accessory_structure_use` (the production path); lock `src/tests/bylaw-accessory-scope.logic.test.ts` (red 2/4 on the production path before the fix, green 4/4) | RATIFIED 2026-10-15 (operator y); (5) 2026-10-16 |
| **M-176** | **MaxBLD answers the maximum-build case (operator ruling 2026-10-15, envelope review Q3; confirms M-172, adds main-wall height).** Operator, verbatim: *"assume the max build case. in terms of the main-wall height if this changes the max build envelope then we need to determine what that is … as per townhouse unit width we just assume no driveway - for the sixplex we just assume the builder would do these things to get the max build size because they make more money"*.<br>**(1) Confirms M-172.** A design choice is taken in the direction that builds more, and the answer discloses it: 10.80.40.1(3) townhouse unit width answers **5.0 m (no individual private driveway)**, the 6.0 m arm is the trade-off disclosure (supersedes M-173(2)'s driveway default); 600.60.40(2)(A) sixplex height answers **10.5 m with its achieved-if conditions**. Site facts stay M-172 (2): never assumed, an "If …" disclosure (shoreline 5.10.40.40, triangular lot 5.10.40.70(5), two lots 10.80.30.10(1)(B), lawfully existing M-158).<br>**(2) Main-wall height is an envelope limit, not a disclosure.** It changes the maximum build: today `enrich_parcels`' max-build pass computes storeys = height ÷ storey height and GFA = LEAST(footprint × storeys, FSI cap) and never reads `main_wall_height_m` (Spec 78 field-inventory row 3 lists it as an input). Measured on the dev DB 2026-10-15: 432,098 of 449,674 parcels with a max build have no FSI cap, so the footprint × storeys box binds; 82,401 of those are at 3+ storeys, where the storey above the main-wall limit sits in the roof. This supersedes M-173(3)'s disclosure treatment for all 8 main-wall pair units (10.10 / 10.20 / 10.40 / 10.80.40.10(2)(A)/(B)); the 8 `design_choice_disclosure.unit_sentences` stay as the interim disclosure until the Spec 78 / `enrich_parcels` change lands.<br>**(3) Correction (started, design `.cursor/mcbylaw/main-wall-height-envelope-2026-10-15.md`):** full storeys = those whose walls fit under the main-wall limit; the storey(s) between the main-wall limit and the height limit count at a roof-storey share, raised by dormers up to the 40 % of main-wall width that 10.x.40.10(5)(B) lets a dormer occupy without becoming a main wall (M-174 (3): the design chooses the dormers, so the maximum takes the 40 %). Lands as a Spec 78 amendment + an `enrich_parcels` max-build change, recaptured in the DB lane. | ACCURATE: the answer is the largest building the law allows, and every assumption that gets there is shown; UNDERSTANDABLE: one rule (build more, disclose how) instead of per-clause choices. | M-172 classes (`vocab.design_choice_disclosure`, `vocab.undetermined_condition_disclosure`); main-wall model: Spec 78 + `scripts/lib/compute/enrich-parcels.js` max-build pass (pending) | RATIFIED 2026-10-15 (operator) |
| **M-177** | **Ancillary and secondary suites: priority and max-build assumptions (operator y 2026-10-16, envelope review of 150.7 / 150.8 / 150.10; applies M-63, M-172, M-176, M-158).** Operator, verbatim: *"Critical - every lot can have one garden suite or laneway suite"*; *"needs separation from our max BLd of 7.5M to achieve 6.3 M height"*; *"We wouldn't know the other buildings - for max build we wouldn't include them"*; *"we will create the max build for the main structure and this will inform the size of the secondary suite"*.<br>**(1) Priority: the suite scenario is pulled into Phase 2 (operator choice (b)).** The A axis (none, garden, laneway; 150.7.20.1(2) = 150.8.20.1(2)) and the P suite variants (detached / semi + secondary suite) are M-63 values. The M-63 note (2026-10-14, Q12-a) projected them out of Layer 2 v1 until a `scenario-classes.json` pin change before P3-5. That timing is amended: the pin change lands in Phase 2 as P2-10 (phase2-plan-v2 §P2-10, "Suite scenario (M-177)"), covering garden, laneway and secondary suites as `principal` / `suite` / `ancillary` structure rows (§S.3). Why: 150.7.60.50(1), 150.7.60.70(1), 150.8.60.50(1) and 150.8.60.70(1)(A) change the principal lot's FSI and coverage answer.<br>**(2) Suite placement is a design choice taken in the build-more direction (M-176 applied).** For A = garden or laneway: suite height takes the 6.3 m arm (150.7.60.40(1)(B), 150.8.60.40(1)(B)) with ≥ 7.5 m separation (150.7.60.30(1)(B), 150.8.60.30(1)(B)) where the lot depth leaves room behind the principal's rear wall for that separation, the suite's depth and its rear setback (a Layer 3 geometry test). Otherwise the suite takes 4.0 m at 4.0 m separation. Storeys 2. GFA = min(120 m², < principal GFA) (60 m² if one storey). Each answer discloses the other arm: "A 6.3 m suite needs 7.5 m from the house; at 4.0 m from the house the suite is limited to 4.0 m."<br>**(3) Restrictive suite design choices are disclosed, not bound (M-172(3)).** Wall openings (150.7.60.20(2)(C), (5)(A); 150.8.60.20(2)(A), (3)(A)), a parking space in the suite (150.7.60.20(4), (6)(B)) and the upper-storey side setback (150.8.60.20(3)(C)) are not applied to the maximum. Other ancillary buildings on the lot are taken as absent (M-158 default, "new build"), so the 20 % / 30 % all-ancillary caps (M-179(1)) bind the suite alone, with the disclosure "If the lot has other ancillary buildings, they count toward the {20 / 30} % ancillary coverage."<br>**(4) Secondary suite size is a split of the principal, not an envelope.** The secondary suite's interior floor area is at most 45 % of the host dwelling unit's (150.10.40.40(1)); a one-storey detached house's suite may take the whole basement (150.10.40.40(2)). This is the Layer 3 field (scenario-scan §5.1 l.251, `avg_unit_gfa_sqm`) computed from the principal's maximum build. One suite per dwelling unit (150.10.20.1(2)) is the class vector's `has_secondary_suite` = 1.<br>**(5) Requirements attached to a suite are report fields.** "No parking required" (150.7.80.1(1), 150.8.80.1(1)(B)), 2 bicycle spaces (150.7.80.1(2), 150.8.80.1(2)) and the landscaping shares (150.7.50.10(1), 150.8.50.10(1)) are Layer 3 report fields (P3-3 inventory) or suite disclosures. They are not envelope limits and add no M-172 class.<br>**Compliance (one line each):** **68** §7.5 rule 1 (class-based applicability), §6 `disclosure`, §11.1 Layer 3 (formulas). **69 / 69a**: M-63 (catalogue; no value added), M-63 note (timing amended by this row, (1)), M-160 (`ancillary_units` stops being 0 for A ≠ none), M-168 (`proposed_height_m` per class), M-172 / M-176 (build-more direction, disclosed), M-158 (absent existing structures), M-66 (parking never a size constraint). **122 / 122a** N/A. **124 / 124a**: declared data (R-BZ); the class pin change is a step-input change under its own WF. **128**: surface shows the suite answer and its disclosures. **129**: oracle C compares base limits; a CoA notice for a suite is a suite-structure item. **Five words:** ACCURATE, the lot's FSI and coverage answer accounts for the suite exclusions; OBSERVABLE, each assumed placement is disclosed; STANDARDIZED, one build-more rule for principal and suite; SCALABLE, class data, no user input; UNDERSTANDABLE, "a 6.3 m suite needs 7.5 m from the house". | The operator ranks the suite rules critical, and four of them change the principal lot's answer. The M-176 rule (build more, disclose how) already decides every suite design choice the review raised. | `scenario-classes.json` pin (A axis, P suite variants) in P2-10 (phase2-plan-v2 §P2-10); structure dimension (§S.3); Layer 3 registry (P3-2/P3-4); `bylaw-rulings` M-177 test (owed, P2-10) | RATIFIED 2026-10-16 (operator y; (1) = option (b)) |

| **M-180** | **Chapter 900 exceptions and houseplexes: the detached-house substitution and "more permissive prevails" (operator y 2026-10-18, family triage MF-1; verdicts on x.30.1(1)/(2) and the x.40.1 "Applicable" / "More Permissive" clauses R (6)/(7), RD (6)/(7), RS (5)/(6), RT (4)/(5), RM (4)/(5)).** Operator, verbatim (2026-10-16): *"the more permissive regulation prevails - this is the benefit of additional housing units. build bigger"*. Text (10.40.40.1(6)): *"if a regulation in clauses 10.40.40.10, 10.40.40.20, 10.40.40.30, 10.40.40.40 or 10.40.40.50 for a detached houseplex or semi-detached houseplex is more permissive than the regulation that would apply … under regulation 10.40.40.1(5), then the more permissive regulation prevails."*<br>**(1) Substitution.** Where a lot's exception (or one in its INCLUDE closure) has a unit for a **detached house** (or a **semi-detached house**) on lot requirements (x.30.1(1)) or principal-building requirements (the x.40.1 "Applicable" clause), that unit also applies to the **detached houseplex** (or the **semi-detached houseplex**, for "the portion of a semi-detached house that constitutes an individual dwelling unit", 10.40.30.1(1)(B)). A declared `vocab.exception_type_alias` (`detached_house → detached_houseplex`, `semi_detached_house → semi_detached_houseplex`) is read by `applies()` for exception-layer units only, on the houseplex classes the zone's clause names (RD: detached only). "The only residential building on a lot" is the new-build default (M-158).<br>**(2) More permissive prevails.** For those classes, on the targets of the articles the zone's clause names (R: 40.10, 40.30, 40.40; RD/RS: 40.10–40.50; RT/RM: 40.10, 40.40; coverage: x.30.40 per x.30.1(2), with R's coverage clause 10.10.60.70(1)(C)), §7.5 rule 4 between the base and exception layers returns **the more permissive** of the two (max bound: the larger; min bound: the smaller). This is the form Spec 68 §7.5 rule 4 already gives a provincial unit (M-55). The PROCEDURAL units carry it as data: `ranks_layers` value `more_permissive: exception, base`, with the article list in `application`, never an id in code.<br>**(3) Disclosure:** "On this exception lot the {measure} is {value} from {clause}: the zone's houseplex rule is more permissive than the exception's detached-house rule ({other value})."<br>**Admission (Spec 68 §5a part 8).** STANDARDIZED: one layer rule, the M-55 form. OBSERVABLE: the M-75 correction records and the rule 4 fixture. RECORDED: this row. SCALABLE: every zone copy (M-151); 77.1 % of residential lots carry an exception (Spec 68 §7.5 note). ACCURATE: the triage probes (`effective()`, generated `displaces[]` attached). SIMPLE: one evaluator branch and data. EXTERNALIZED: no number; the article list is data. | Five zones say "the more permissive regulation prevails". FSI is `not_applicable` for the houseplex and for the house with a suite (M-182, M-183). **(2) Basement** (`renovation_basement`). Basement floor area may be excluded from GFA (10.5.40.40(3)(A)). *"A dwelling unit may not be entirely below … established grade"* (5.10.1.30(3); keyed PROHIBIT `not_modelled`, disclosed). The main pedestrian entrance is ≤ 1.2 m above established grade: R 10.10.40.10(6), RD 10.20.40.10(6), RS 10.40.40.10(4), RM 10.80.40.10(4); RT has none. It governs the main entrance, not a basement door. A level stays a basement while its floor-to-joist midpoint is below established grade (800.50(80)); otherwise it is a storey in GFA. In 600.60 the 10.5 m houseplex height needs the lowest level raised 1.0–1.5 m (600.60.40(2)(A)). Basement headroom is a Building Code matter, disclosed, never evaluated.<br>**Admission.** STANDARDIZED: M-169 disclosures. OBSERVABLE: Spec 78 P5.12 codes. RECORDED: this row. SCALABLE: all zones. | **M-202** | **Houseplex unit cap, corrected (verified review finding; corrects M-194).** (1) **A semi-detached houseplex is capped at 4**: 800.50(746)(A) *"no more than four dwelling units"*; a sixplex is *"a building located on a single lot"* and *"a type of detached houseplex"* (600.60.20(1)(A)(iv)); 600.60.40(3)(D) bars converting a semi to five or six. (2) **600.60.40(1)(A) lifts a 'u' below 6 to 6 only in R, RT and RM**: it lists *"10.10.40.1(3)(A), 10.60.40.1(2)(A) and 10.80.40.1(2)(A)"*; in RD and RS the 'u' cap stands. (3) **No 'u' label means no 'u' cap** (the cap is then 4 / 6 and floors), never `not_evaluated` (the evaluator defect is M-220). (4) **RT 10.60.40.1(2)(A) is the 'u' cap** (*"the numerical value following the letter "u" is the maximum number of dwelling units permitted on the lot"*), not a one-building rule; RT and RM have no "one residential building" clause (x.40.80(2) governs two or more buildings, M-187 Q12). So `dwelling_units ≤ min(u [lifted to 6 only in R/RT/RM inside 600.60], 4 for S always, 6 iff gis_in and detached, floors)`.<br>**Admission.** STANDARDIZED: the D7 form of M-194. OBSERVABLE: INV-11. | **M-203** | **The attic test decides inclusion in GFA; a failing attic is free floor area, not zero (verified review finding; corrects Spec 78 P5.2 item 6).** 10.5.40.40(1): *"the gross floor area … includes the portion of the floor area in an attic that has a vertical clearance of more than 1.4 metres …, if at least 80% of the area has: (A) a vertical clearance of more than 2.0 metres; and (B) an area of at least 10.0 square metres"*. An attic that fails the test still has floor area; it is not counted toward FSI. So form (b) reports two figures: counted attic GFA (passes) and `attic_outside_gfa` livable area (fails), and where FSI binds the failing attic is livable area outside the cap. It is a storey either way (800.50(820)) unless a clause excludes it (RD 10.20.40.10(8)). Disclosure `attic_outside_gfa`.<br>**Admission.** STANDARDIZED (one geometry), OBSERVABLE (P5.13 vector), RECORDED, SCALABLE (all zones), ACCURATE (verbatim), SIMPLE, EXTERNALIZED (literals keyed under O-7). | Reality check §2 "Form (b) attic test WRONG (misapplied)". | P5.13 (**OWED, ①**); attic-test keying (**OWED, keying lane**) | RATIFIED 2026-10-18 (operator item 5; compliance table `.cursor/maxbld-fold5-compliance-2026-10-18.md`) |

(2) **An RM houseplex has no depth or length cap**: 10.80.40.20(1) and 10.80.40.30(1) name only *"a detached house or a semi-detached house"* (`not_applicable`, M-183 form). | **M-205** | **The main building's setbacks are explicit, by-law-derived fields (verified review findings).** (1) **Front:** 10.5.40.70(1) averaging (*"the average of the front yard setbacks of those buildings on the abutting lots"*, buildings ≤ 15.0 m from the lot) applies wherever `parcel_abutments` holds the abutting buildings; x.40.70(1)'s 6.0 m applies only *"If regulation 10.5.40.70(1) does not apply"*. The averaged value uses the estimated neighbour setbacks (`front_setback_est_m`; Q-B1 advisory), so its basis is disclosed (`front_yard_averaging`) and its trust tier is `estimate`. (2) **Sides:** R x.40.70(3) with the **0.45 m blank-wall reduction** 10.10.40.70(4) (*"may be reduced to 0.45 metres if there are no windows or doors in that side of the building"*), taken by default as the main-building analogue of LC-35 / M-197 (`blank_side_wall` discloses 0.9 m with windows); RD/RS by required frontage; **RD 10.20.40.70(5)** 7.5 m only for the part of the building beyond the lesser of 17.0 m from the front main wall or 19.0 m from the required front yard (lots whose required frontage > 18.0 m), not a whole-lot side yard; RD (6) corner 3.0 m; RT 0.9 m; RM 1.2 / 1.5 m. (3) **Rear:** the greater of 7.5 m and 25 % of lot depth (RD/RS/RM, M-174(5)); R/RT 7.5 m. (4) **Depth / length caps** with the M-204 releases. (5) **Lane centreline:** 10.5.40.70(2) (*"no closer than 2.5 metres from the original centreline of a lane"*) for the main building and **10.5.60.20(4)** for ancillary buildings, which 150.7.60.20 / 150.8.60.20 do not displace (they displace 10.5.60.20(2),(3),(5),(6) only); the current centreline stands in for the original (`lane_centreline_original`).<br>**Admission.** STANDARDIZED: M-176 / M-197. | | *Dated note 2026-10-19 (M-205(1); operator ruling "looks great!", revising the same-day tight-threshold ruling; Spec 131 LC-58; Spec 130 Q-B1):* where 10.5.40.70(1) averaging applies (one or two qualifying neighbours, every estimate `est_status` `estimated`), the front setback is **max(averaged estimate + `fya_porch_allowance_m`, the zone's x.40.70(1) default)**, default allowance 1.0 m; the lot is always flagged "needs survey" and `front_yard_needs_survey` is disclosed (mandatory, high importance). The estimates are measured to building outlines that take in porches and steps, so they run short. Excluded estimates (`no_building`, `no_frontage_street`, `street_not_found`, `estimate_negative`, `estimate_out_of_bounds`) and three or more neighbours (Q-B2) keep the zone default, "needs survey", `front_yard_not_measured`; no qualifying neighbour, the zone default alone. Basis: n = 219 CoA notices citing averaging: the estimate is at or below the examiner's figure 89.0 % of the time (median error 1.10 m, 26.9 % within 0.5 m); max(estimate + 1.0 m, 6.0 m) has mean error 1.39 m, 54.8 % within 1 m, and is below the examiner's figure (too generous) on 22.4 % (20.5 % by more than 0.05 m); 6.0 m alone: mean error 2.53 m, too generous on 63.9 %. Resolved on read by `scripts/analysis/bylaw/front-averaging.mjs`. *Amended 2026-10-20 by M-245 (operator chat CHAT-21): no zone-default floor; the zone default applies only where averaging does not.* |

| **M-209** | **Laneway eligibility uses all three arms of 150.8.30.20(1), measured from the centreline (verified review finding; refines KFM-78-9).** *"A laneway suite must be on a lot with a rear lot line or side lot line abutting a lane for at least 3.5 metres; or on a lot with a cumulative total of at least 3.5 metres along a side lot line and rear lot line"*. Arms: rear ≥ 3.5 m; side ≥ 3.5 m; side + rear cumulative ≥ 3.5 m. The measure is `lane_abutting_length_m` per edge: the parcel boundary within `lane_buffer_m` of a `toronto_centreline` Laneway segment, each edge classed rear / side by its nearest centreline (reality check §3.C: 75 % of `abuts_laneway` parcels pass, so the 20 m flag overstates eligibility by ≈ 25 %). With no lane width the buffer is assumed, so fidelity is `differs` and disclosed. The 20 m flag is never the test.<br>**Admission.** STANDARDIZED, OBSERVABLE (INV-15), RECORDED, SCALABLE, ACCURATE (measured sample), SIMPLE, EXTERNALIZED (3.5 m keyed; buffer a logic variable). | Reality check §3.C; operator verdict "REAR LOT LINE: 2 possibilities". | `lane_abutting_length_m` derivation (**OWED, Spec 130 / enrich_centreline WF3**); INV-15 (**OWED, ①**) | RATIFIED 2026-10-18 (operator item 5; compliance table `.cursor/maxbld-fold5-compliance-2026-10-18.md`) |

| | *Dated note 2026-10-19 (operator, Q-MS-1/Q-MS-2):* **"A lot abutting a major street" is read as: a lot line of the lot touches the street's right-of-way, whether it is the front lot line or a side lot line.** 800.50(457) defines Major Street by the Policy Areas Overlay Map; 800.50 does not define "abut", so its plain meaning applies (800.50(825): a street is a public right-of-way; 800.50(275): the front lot line divides a lot from a street). The map is held as centrelines (`zoning_policy_road_overlay`), so `parcels.on_policy_road` is derived as: the lot's primary frontage street (enrich_centreline) has the nearby line's name within `enrich_parcels_major_street_frontage_m` (20 m), **or** it is a corner / through lot within `enrich_parcels_major_street_flank_m` (13 m) of the line (Spec 65 §2 step 7; migration 277). The 5 m buffer it replaces flagged 21 of 440,093 residential lots; the queued buffer-only plan is retired (Q-MS-3). Every major-street reading (M-187 F06, M-214 (1), Spec 67 NF-10) uses this flag. | `.cursor/major-street-audit-2026-10-19.md`; validation report D5 | Spec 65 §2 step 7 | RATIFIED 2026-10-19 (operator) |

| **M-224** | **Coxwell area: the generous (purposive) reading of 150.7.60.30(1)(C) (operator 2026-10-18: "Generous reading now", option (c) of `.cursor/coxwell-review-2026-10-18.md`; amends M-193).** Inside the 150.7.60.30(1)(C) area (*"Coxwell Avenue, Coxwell Boulevard and Massey Creek to the west, the Don River and Sunrise Avenue to the north, Victoria Park Avenue and Nursewood Park to the east, and Lake Ontario to the south"*), a garden suite taller than 4.0 m needs only **5.0 m** separation, and its height steps by the **actual** separation: **6.3 m is allowed at ≥ 5.0 m in the area**. Outside the area nothing changes: 4.0 m below 7.5 m, 6.3 m at ≥ 7.5 m (150.7.60.40(1)). Basis: (C) is the specific clause and would otherwise do nothing for a compliant building (the presumption against surplusage); Council's intent also shows in exception 900.2.10(736)(A)(iv). **Risk: medium** — an examiner may apply 150.7.60.40(1)(A) (*"located less than 7.5 metres from the residential building"* → 4.0 m) literally. **Mandatory disclosure** on every lot that takes this reading (`coxwell_area`): "The City may read the 4.0 m height cap as applying below 7.5 m from the house; a written City interpretation is pending." **Polygon:** needed (`bylaw_map_areas`, Spec 58 lane; M-193). Until it exists, membership is unknown, so every lot takes the literal reading, and lots plausibly inside the bounding streets get `coxwell_may_qualify`: "This lot may be in the Coxwell–Victoria Park area, where a suite over 4.0 m may sit 5.0 m from the house." The coarse filter is for that disclosure only: the parcel centroid lies east of the westernmost `toronto_centreline` vertex named Coxwell Ave / Coxwell Blvd, west of the easternmost vertex named Victoria Park Ave, and south of the northernmost vertex named Sunrise Ave (over-inclusive by design; never an answer). **Switch:** the reading is the logic variable `compute_maxbld_scenarios_coxwell_generous_reading` (1 = generous, 0 = literal); if the City answers no, a ruling sets it to 0 and the literal reading returns with no code change.<br>**Admission.** STANDARDIZED: the 600.60 map-area form + an M-176 disclosure. OBSERVABLE: `coxwell_area` / `coxwell_may_qualify` counts; P5.13 vector "in the area, 5.0 m → 6.3 m; outside, 5.0 m → 4.0 m". RECORDED: this row. SCALABLE: one area, one clause. ACCURATE: verbatim; the risk is stated. SIMPLE: one coupled rule. EXTERNALIZED: 5.0 m keyed; the switch is a logic variable. | The Coxwell review (2026-10-18): the literal reading never over-states; the generous reading adds ≈ 45–60 m² on R ≈ 12–15 m area lots. | the polygon (**OWED, Spec 58 lane**); one written City question, which would also settle exception 900.2.10(736) (**OWED, operator / City**); P5.5 coupled rule + P5.13 vector (**OWED, ①**); keyer A's (C) disclosure text ("needs only 5.0 m (not 7.5 m)") re-worded to the mandatory disclosure above (**OWED, keying lane**; a note only) | RATIFIED 2026-10-18 (operator) |

| **M-228** | **Through lot: the front lot line is the street line the neighbouring lots front on (operator 2026-10-20, "1 & 2 y"; drafted from review answers batch 5; renumbered from the draft id M-226, which LC-59 (4) already cites for Q-PR-2).** 10.5.30.20(1): *"Despite regulation 5.10.30.20(2), on a through lot in the Residential Zone category, any lot line separating the lot from a street may be selected as the front lot line, if: (A) the lot line is not separated from the street by a 0.3 metre reserve; (B) the lot line abuts a street where an adjacent lot has its front lot line on the same street"*. Operator on (A)/(B), "wrong": *"Through Lots - we fall for this … It needs to match the adjacent lot."* No ruling covers it: the units are `not_modelled` (`needs_data_we_lack`), and M-198(1) covers only the suite rear setback on a through lot.<br>**Ruled.** (1) On a through lot (`parcels.is_through_lot`, data-filled, M-169(3)) the front lot line is the street line on which the adjacent lots front, read as the lot's own address street (`parcels.primary_frontage_street_name`): neighbours on the same block face share it in the common case. The opposite street line is the rear lot line, and the front, rear and street setbacks follow from that choice. (2) (A) the 0.3 m reserve is a site fact not held: taken as not separating (M-169(1)). (3) Disclosed by a new P5.12 code `through_lot_front_line`: "This is a through lot; the front lot line is taken on {street}, the street the neighbouring lots face. If the City sets the other street line as the front, the front and rear setbacks swap." (4) (C) (townhouse / apartment on a major street) stays deferred (M-214(3)).<br>**Alternatives.** (b) Keep (A)/(B) disclosed only and leave the front line to the address frontage without saying so (today's implicit behaviour; the operator called it wrong). (c) Evaluate both street lines and take the larger build (M-176 form): contradicts (B), "it needs to match the adjacent lot".<br>**Admission (V3).** STANDARDIZED: the M-169 site-fact default plus one closed code. OBSERVABLE: `through_lot_front_line` count; P5.13 vector (a through lot, both street names). RECORDED: this row. SCALABLE: every through lot from two parcel columns. ACCURATE: verbatim (B); the proxy (address street = neighbours' front street) is stated, and its miss case (a corner-through or reversed-frontage block) is the disclosure. SIMPLE: one designation. EXTERNALIZED: the street choice reads parcel data, no code constant. **Ruled y** (operator 2026-10-20); applied to 10.5.30.20(1)(A)/(B) by keyed corrections, disclosed `through_lot_front_line` (Spec 78 P5.12). | Review answers batch 5: 10.5.30.20(1)(A), (B) "wrong". | keyed corrections + P5.12 code (applied; `bylaw-answers-batch-5.logic.test.ts`); Layer 3 lot-line classification (Spec 78 P5.5, ML-1; **OWED, step commit ①**); pure function `throughLotFrontLine` in the P5.7 shared module `src/features/leads/lib/renovation-shared.js`, locked by `renovation-shared.logic.test.ts` › "M-228 — …" (green 2026-10-20; red evidence `docs/reports/red-evidence/bylaw/renovation-shared-m228-m229-red.json`); its wiring into the step (**OWED, step commit ①**) | RULED 2026-10-20 (operator, "1 & 2 y": Q-through-lot-front) |
| **M-229** | **Mechanical attic area is left out of GFA by default (operator 2026-10-20, "1 & 2 y"; drafted from review answers batch 5; amends M-171 for this one deduction; renumbered from the draft id M-227).** 10.5.40.40(2): *"If the floor area meets the conditions of regulation 10.5.40.40(1) and the area or portion of the area is used for mechanical equipment for the functional operation of the building, that area is not included in the gross floor area of the building if it is not more than 5% of the permitted maximum gross floor area of the building to a maximum of 20 square metres."* Operator, "wrong": *"Every build has this - this should be factored in our GFA's - the default is 5% of the max GFA can be allocated to this as long as it doesn't exceed 20 sq meters. Every building has this. this should be a factor."* M-171 today discloses every GFA deduction that needs building facts and never applies it, so this needs a ruling.<br>**Ruled.** (1) Where the maximum design has an attic that passes the 10.5.40.40(1) test (counted in GFA, M-203), up to min(5 % × permitted maximum GFA, 20 m²) of it is taken as mechanical-equipment area and left out of GFA: a permissive design choice taken as met (M-172(3)), as M-210 already applies the same clause to suite GFA. (2) The answer carries an achieved-if sentence: "{X} m² of the attic is taken as mechanical-equipment space, which the City leaves out of the floor-area total (GFA)." (3) No counted attic (flat-roof form, or an attic that fails the test and is already outside GFA, M-203): no change. A basement mechanical room is already outside GFA (10.5.40.40(3)(A), `basement_excluded`). (4) The 5 % and 20 m² are clause literals (Layer 2 keying of 10.5.40.40(2), today `not_modelled`), never code constants.<br>**Alternative.** (b) Keep M-171: disclose only (`gfa_deductions_not_applied`), the maximum unchanged.<br>**Admission (V3).** STANDARDIZED: the M-172 achieved-if form (the M-210 suite twin). OBSERVABLE: P5.13 vector (a pitched-roof lot where FSI binds: GFA cap + min(5 %, 20 m²) of attic). RECORDED: this row. SCALABLE: every lot with a counted attic. ACCURATE: verbatim; applies only where the attic counts. SIMPLE: one deduction, one sentence. M-102 (*"mixed bound directions or measures in one unit — the unit is UNUSUAL"*) and M-106 (*"mixes bound directions, which one unit `bound` cannot carry"*): amended for targets that declare their own bound. (3) An absence ruling may carry `answer: not_applicable` with `subject_clause` (the clause whose subject excludes the type); it then returns `not_applicable:<subject_clause>` instead of `unregulated`. (4) New rulings, each executed against its pinned page as ABS-2 is: ABS-3 R `building_length_m` (10.10.40.20 holds only (1)(A) townhouse and (1)(B) apartment); ABS-4 RT `building_length_m` (10.60.40.20(1)(A)/(B)); ABS-5 RT `building_depth_m` (no 10.60.40.30); ABS-6 RD semi-detached house `building_depth_m` (10.20.40.30 names detached / houseplex only); ABS-7 RM detached houseplex `building_length_m` / `building_depth_m` / `main_wall_height_m` → `not_applicable` (10.80.40.20(1), 10.80.40.30(1), 10.80.40.10(2): *"a detached house or a semi-detached house"*). M-54 (*"The R zone has no principal coverage regulation, so it stays `no_candidate` (no clause to carry)"*): amended as quoted above; ABS-1 carries the clause. | **M-295** | **`effective()` returns a structured `losers[]` beside the free-text trace (operator approved 2026-10-21 on the rulings page, completed "completed teh rulings - proceed", `2026-10-21-page-4d-c#CHAT-38.L2-LOSERS`; Spec 134:44, :121, :171; Phase 2 plan v2:192; Layer 2 plan PIN L2-PREREQ).** A value result (and a whole-set DISAPPLY `not_applicable` result) carries `losers[]` of `{unit_id, dropped_by, by}` (`rule2_arg_displaced` adds `arg`). `dropped_by` is one of the eight members of the exported `DROPPED_BY`: `rule2_displaced` (a same-layer "Despite" drops the unit), `rule2_arg_displaced` (one argument of the unit is replaced or removed; the unit may still win, edited), `rule3_outranked` (a unit that displaces the precedence rule outranks a higher layer), `rule4_higher_layer` (the highest layer replaces the lower, per bound), `rule4_provincial_restraint` (a provincial unit restrains a more restrictive by-law value), `rule4a_less_restrictive` (the most restrictive same-layer value governs, including unlimited or unregulated losing to a finite value), `condition_false` (a value unit whose condition does not hold; `by` null), `order_tiebreak` (equal values; the first in declared order stands). Purely additive: no existing field changes; the eval-perf golden is hashed with `losers` left out and keeps its pin. Where building types (M-50) or undecided resolutions (M-60) are merged, `losers` keeps only the entries every resolution agrees on.
